## Phase 16 — Per-renderer rollout matrix (`MOD-1600`–`MOD-1698`)

→ CNAEXT.md §6. EasyGL lands each subsystem first (Phases 1–15); this phase carries it to the other
renderers. **Every row here is independently schedulable and never blocks the reference work (D4).**
Each row's acceptance criterion is the same shape: *the subsystem's golden/reference test from its own
phase passes on this renderer, or the renderer reports `false` from the matching capability and its
documented fallback is verified.*

### 16.1 Float render targets (→ N12)

Tiers follow `CNA::GraphicsRendererType` on `next` (49 identities). The 2D-only, fixed-function and
web-DOM identities are handled once in §16.6 rather than repeated per subsystem.

| ID | Renderer | Status | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| MOD-1600 | Vulkan | 🟨 | **Measured, not implemented.** A real Vulkan build now exists in this environment (`cmake-build-vulkan`, Mesa lavapipe reporting Vulkan 1.4), and the renderer's own capability dump says `SurfaceFormat: Color only (Task 176)` — so float render targets are genuinely absent rather than untested, and `RenderTarget2D` refuses the format instead of substituting `Color`, which is the documented fallback working. Implementing `VK_FORMAT_R16G16B16A16_SFLOAT` attachments is still the open work. |
| MOD-1601 | SdlGpu | 🟨 | **Measured, not implemented.** A real SDL_GPU build now exists here (`cmake-build-sdlgpu`, needs `libshaderc-dev`), and `cna_test_cnaext_caps` on it reports `FloatRenderTargets: no`, `HalfFloatRenderTargets: no` — so the base class's false-by-default answer stands and `RenderTarget2D` refuses the format rather than substituting `Color`, which is the documented fallback working. Implementing float attachments on `SDL_GPUTextureFormat_R16G16B16A16_FLOAT` is the open work. |
| MOD-1602 | Bgfx | 🟨 | **Measured, not implemented — after fixing what stopped it building.** `BgfxRendererDescriptor.cpp` had one closing brace too many after `ResolvedWindowKind()`, so the anonymous namespace closed twice and the identity had never compiled — the **third** file damaged by the same merge, `2f00c201`, after OpenGL1 and LLGL. With it fixed, bgfx runs on its OpenGL backend (`active renderer: OpenGL 2.1` on llvmpipe) and reports `FloatRenderTargets: no`, `HalfFloatRenderTargets: no`; the refusal path runs. |
| MOD-1603 | WebGPU | 🟨 | **Measured, not implemented.** The native `wgpu-native` build runs here (Vulkan on lavapipe) and reports `FloatRenderTargets: no`, `HalfFloatRenderTargets: no`; the refusal path runs. `rgba16float` is core in WebGPU, so the row's own note still holds — this is the easiest one left, not a boundary. |
| MOD-1604 | D3D11 | 🟨 | **Measured on a real D3D11 device, under Wine.** The Windows cross-build now works end to end here (see the row's own note for what it took), and `CnaTests.exe` reports the renderer initialising at feature level `0xB100` with the debug layer on. Float render targets: `no`. The refusal path is what runs. |
| MOD-1605 | D3D12 | 🟨 | **Better than the row expected: it runs here, on a real device.** `D3D12 device resources created; feature level 0xB100, tearing supported, swap chain available` under Wine. Float render targets: `no`; the refusal path runs. Two toolchain fixes were needed to get it compiling at all — see `MOD-1625`. |
| MOD-1606 | D3D9 | 🟨 | **Measured on a real D3D9 device under Wine** (`VertexShaderVersion=0xFFFE0300`, `PixelShaderVersion=0xFFFF0300` — SM3 both ways). Float render targets: `no`, so the refusal path runs. `D3DFMT_A16B16G16R16F` and the no-blending caveat remain the shape of any future implementation. |
| MOD-1607 | D3D10 | 🟨 | **Measured on a real D3D10 device under Wine.** Float render targets: `no`; the refusal path runs. D3D10 has `DXGI_FORMAT_R16G16B16A16_FLOAT` natively, so this is implementable rather than a boundary. |
| MOD-1608 | OpenGL4 | 🟨 | **Measured, not implemented.** `cna_test_cnaext_caps` on a real OPENGL4 build reports `FloatRenderTargets: no`, `HalfFloatRenderTargets: no`, so the false-by-default answer stands and `RenderTarget2D` refuses the format rather than substituting `Color`. `GL_RGBA16F` attachments are core in GL 4.x, so this is implementable work rather than a boundary. |
| MOD-1609 | OpenGL2 | 🟨 | **Measured, not implemented — and this is OpenGL2's only row, so it carries the whole picture.** Both float capabilities `no`; `CustomEffects: yes` with `ExecutesShaderSourceEXT: no` (it accepts an effect and does not run this layer's GLSL, so every shader-based pass reports `isSupported() == false` and copies through); `Instancing: yes` with `MultiStreamVertexInput: no`, the mismatch `MOD-1621`'s fix covers; shadow sampling, IBL and compute all `no`. Engine-layer suites: **406 pass · 85 skip · 0 fail**. `ARB_texture_float` is the route for float targets if it is ever taken; nothing has declined it, it simply has not been done. |
| MOD-1610 | Magnum | 🟨 | **Measured, not implemented.** `FloatRenderTargets: no`, `HalfFloatRenderTargets: no` on desktop GL 4.5 (Mesa llvmpipe); the refusal path runs. Magnum exposes `GL::TextureFormat::RGBA16F`, so this is implementable work. |
| MOD-1611 | Diligent | 🟨 | **Measured on one native API, not two.** DiligentCore picks its device at runtime and here it selects **Vulkan** on Mesa lavapipe (`llvmpipe (LLVM 20.1.2)`, API 1.4.318) — D3D11/D3D12 need Windows and Metal needs macOS, so "at least two" is not reachable in this container. On that device: `FloatRenderTargets: no`, `HalfFloatRenderTargets: no`, and the refusal path runs. |
| MOD-1612 | LLGL | 🟨 | **Measured, not implemented — after fixing what stopped it building at all.** `LlglRendererDescriptor.cpp` carried a headless `namespace { return 0; } }` block, the remains of a `PrepareWindowFlags()` helper the PLAT-8 merge deleted the signature of, so `-DCNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERER=LLGL` had never compiled (the same merge, `2f00c201`, that broke OpenGL1). With it removed: `FloatRenderTargets: no`, `HalfFloatRenderTargets: no`, and the refusal path runs. |
| MOD-1613 | Sokol | 🟨 | **Measured, not implemented.** `FloatRenderTargets: no`, `HalfFloatRenderTargets: no`; the refusal path runs. sokol_gfx has `SG_PIXELFORMAT_RGBA16F`, so this is implementable work. |
| MOD-1614 | Metal | ⛔ | **macOS only, by a hard configure gate.** `-DCNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERER=METAL` fails immediately on Linux: *"METAL renderer is currently supported only on macOS; iOS and tvOS remain unvalidated."* There is no cross-compilation route — Metal needs the macOS SDK and a Metal device — so this is refused with the reason rather than left open as if untried. |
| MOD-1615 | FNA3D | 🟨 | **Builds, cannot run here.** The FNA3D renderer compiles and links against the vendored FNA3D, but `FNA3D_CreateDevice` fails in this container for every driver — *"no FNA3D driver (SDL_GPU, Direct3D 11 or OpenGL) could create a device for this window"* — and forcing one with `FNA3D_FORCE_DRIVER=OpenGL` or `=SDL_GPU` changes nothing. With no device, every one of the 493 filtered tests fails at construction rather than reporting a capability, so there is nothing to measure. Same shape as `OPENGLES1`: an environment limitation stated rather than a CNA finding. |
| MOD-1616 | Wicked | ⛔ | **Blocked on an external checkout this container does not have.** `-DCNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERER=WICKED` fails at configure: `CNA_WICKED_ROOT` is empty and `CNA_WICKED_AUTO_FETCH=OFF`, so it wants a Wicked Engine clone pinned at `27c0df16` supplied by hand. Cloning and building a whole second engine to measure four capability answers is not proportionate to the row; the blocker and the exact recipe are recorded instead. |
| MOD-1617 | PortableGL | ⛔ | **Decided and documented, from a measurement rather than a guess** (`MOD-1692`'s run): PortableGL reports `FloatRenderTargets: no` and `HalfFloatRenderTargets: no`, and it stays that way. It is a CPU rasterizer whose whole point is an OpenGL 3.x-shaped API in portable C; float colour attachments would be real work in its own framebuffer code for a renderer that also answers `no` to shader-source execution, so nothing downstream of the HDR target could use them. The documented fallback — `RenderTarget2D` refusing the format rather than substituting `Color` — is what runs, and it runs correctly. |

### 16.2 Post-process passes (tonemap/bloom/SSAO/FXAA) (→ N25)

| ID | Renderer | Status | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| MOD-1620 | Vulkan | 🟨 | **Measured; blocked by a concrete mismatch.** The Vulkan renderer reports `CustomEffects` true, but `VulkanEffectRenderer::CompileProgram` takes **SPIR-V bytecode**, while every engine-layer pass hands it GLSL source — so all of them fail to compile with `SPIR-V size must be a multiple of 4 bytes` and the chain copies through. The frame is still correct (`cnaext_bloom_test` asserts the inert path pixel for pixel), and the example now SKIPs with that reason instead of reporting four failures. Unblocking this needs either GLSL→SPIR-V compilation in that renderer or a shader-language question the passes can ask. |
| MOD-1621 | SdlGpu | 🟨 | **Measured, and it found an engine-layer bug.** SDL_GPU reports `CustomEffects: yes` but `ExecutesShaderSourceEXT: no`, so every shader-based pass correctly reports `isSupported() == false` and copies through — 0 failures across the whole engine-layer suite there. What it *did* catch is `InstancedRendererEXT`: SDL_GPU answers `Instancing: yes` (the base class's `default: return true`) while `DrawInstancedPrimitives` is the base class's refusal and `MultiStreamVertexInput` is `no`. `isInstancingSupported()` asked only the first capability, so `draw()` threw where it should have taken the per-instance fallback. Fixed by asking **both** — the instanced path binds the transforms as a *second* vertex stream, so it needs multi-stream input as much as it needs instancing. `TrianglePart.OneDrawCallForEveryInstanceOrAnExplicitRefusal` now passes on both renderers. **Renderer-level finding, not fixed here:** SDL_GPU's `Instancing` answer is a promise it does not keep, and correcting it belongs to `plan_sdlgpu.md` rather than to an engine-layer commit. |
| MOD-1622 | Bgfx | 🟨 | **Measured:** `CustomEffects: yes` / `ExecutesShaderSourceEXT: no` — the `MOD-1699` pair again — so every shader-based pass reports `isSupported() == false` and copies through: **402 pass · 89 skip · 0 fail**. No `shaderc` regeneration was needed to reach that: the engine layer's passes never get as far as bgfx's own shader pipeline, because the two-part question stops them first. Regeneration is what *implementing* them would need. |
| MOD-1623 | WebGPU | 🟨 | **Measured, and it found two engine-layer bugs — the most productive renderer in this sweep.** WebGPU answers `CustomEffects: yes` / `ExecutesShaderSourceEXT: no` (as expected while `WEBGPU-76` is open), and the suites end at **405 pass · 86 skip · 0 fail** *after* both fixes. **(1) `DepthNormalPrepass` trusted the `MultipleRenderTargets` capability** — the `MOD-1699` lesson in a fourth guise. WebGPU reports that capability and then throws `"multiple simultaneous render targets are not implemented on this renderer yet"` from `SetRenderTargets`, so `begin()` threw where the class's own two-pass fallback would have worked. It now **probes the bind once at construction** and falls back; its test compares against what was bound rather than what was claimed, because comparing against the claim fails on exactly the renderer the fallback exists for. **(2) `EffectPassTest.AnAdaptedEffectRunsInsideAChain` gated on `CustomEffects` alone** and hit `"custom SpriteBatch effects are not implemented yet"`; it now asks the two-part question. |
| MOD-1624 | D3D11 | 🟨 | **Measured: 402 pass · 86 skip · 0 fail** on the engine-layer suites, through Wine on a real D3D11 device — the first time this plan's tests have run on a Windows renderer. Getting there needed two fixes outside the engine layer, both recorded in their own commits: **eleven POSIX `setenv` call sites** had crept back into shared test files after `docs/cnatests-mingw-setenv-proposal.md` removed all 62, so `CnaTests.exe` would not compile (now fixed and gated by `CNAEXT_NoPosixSetenv`); and **sharp-runtime's `Environment.cpp` guards `_dupenv_s` with `defined(_WIN32)`**, which MinGW-w64 satisfies without exporting the symbol — `undefined reference to __imp__dupenv_s`. The correct guard is `defined(_MSC_VER)`. That is a **one-line fix in a different repository**, applied locally to obtain this measurement and **not committed anywhere**: without it, no Windows cross-build of `CnaTests` links at all. |
| MOD-1625 | D3D12 | 🟨 | **Measured: 426 selected · 323 pass · 86 skip · 0 fail — and 17 tests that kill the process.** `CustomEffects: yes` / `ExecutesShaderSourceEXT: no`: D3D12's `ShaderEffect` compiles HLSL, so the layer's GLSL fails at `#version` (`W4303: Ignoring unknown directive`) and every shader-based pass reports `isSupported() == false` and copies through. Two things needed recording. **(1) A renderer defect, `plan_dx.md`'s to fix, not this layer's:** D3D12 **segfaults (139) on the copy-through path itself** -- a `FullscreenPass::draw` into a `RenderTarget2D`, which is the fallback *every* unsupported pass takes. The 17 casualties are exactly the tests that reach it -- `BlitPassTest`/`EffectPassTest` directly, and every `RenderPipelineTest`/`DeviceLossTest`/`MultiDeviceTest` case that enables a pass. Measuring the other 409 needed a driver that resumes past each crash with the remaining tests as its filter, rather than one run that stops at the first. The pass tests themselves do not crash because the shader-execution gate skips them first, which is why this reads as 0 failures rather than 17. **(2) A gap in this layer's gating, fixed here:** `EnvironmentProcessorTest.PrefilteringKeepsMipZeroSharpAndFlattensTheLast` and `…GenerationCostIsLoadTimeWork` failed with `TextureCube::SetData: this graphics renderer did not store the complete requested cube face region`. Both were gated on `CanStoreCubeFaces`, which D3D12 **passes** -- it round-trips an unmipped face and then refuses `SetData` into mip 1. The specular prefilter writes one roughness per mip, so it needs the narrower question; a third probe `CanStoreCubeMipLevels` / `CNA_SKIP_WITHOUT_CUBE_MIP_STORAGE` now asks it. Verified both ways: still **OK** on EasyGL, now **SKIPPED** on D3D12. |
| MOD-1626 | D3D9 | 🟨 | **Measured: 489 ran · 401 pass · 87 skip · 0 engine-layer failures.** The single failure in the run is `ContextRecoveryTest.PartialUpdateAfterShadowFreedThrowsInsteadOfCorruptingTexture`, an **XNA-layer** test that only matched the filter through the word *Shadow* in its name; D3D9 answers `SetContextRecoveryEnabled not yet implemented`, which is `plan_dx9.md`'s business, not this layer's. No pass reached SM3's limits, because none of them ran: the two-part question stops them first. |
| MOD-1627 | D3D10 | 🟨 | **Measured: 476 ran · 375 pass · 101 skip · 0 fail.** The highest skip count of any 3D renderer measured, which is the capability gates doing their job rather than a gap in the run: D3D10's own scope is deliberately bounded (`docs/directx10-renderer.md`), so more subsystems report `isSupported() == false` here than anywhere else and every one of them copies through instead of failing. |
| MOD-1628 | OpenGL4 | 🟨 | **Measured, and it is the interesting one.** OPENGL4 answers `CustomEffects: yes` and `ExecutesShaderSourceEXT: no` — it accepts an effect and does not run this layer's GLSL. That is exactly the pair `MOD-1699` exists for, and the two-part question does its job: every shader-based pass reports `isSupported() == false` and copies through, giving **406 pass · 85 skip · 0 fail**. It also answers `Instancing: yes` with `MultiStreamVertexInput: no`, the same mismatch SDL_GPU has, which `MOD-1621`'s fix already covers. |
| MOD-1629 | Magnum | 🟨 | **Measured, and it needed the same guard Sokol did.** `CustomEffects: yes` / `ExecutesShaderSourceEXT: no` — the `MOD-1699` pair — so every shader-based pass reports `isSupported() == false` and copies through: **410 pass · 88 skip · 0 fail**. Magnum is also the only renderer measured so far that answers `MultiStreamVertexInput: yes` alongside `Instancing: yes`, so its instanced path is the one `MOD-1621`'s two-part question actually admits. Before that could be measured it **aborted the test process**: Magnum keeps the active GL context in one process-wide global and aborts with `GL::Context: Another context currently active` on a second one. `Magnum::CreateGraphicsRenderer` now refuses by name, before anything is constructed — the third renderer in this sweep needing exactly that (TinyGL already had it, Sokol and Magnum did not). |
| MOD-1630 | Diligent | 🟨 | **Measured:** Diligent is the first renderer measured here that answers `CustomEffects: no` outright while still rasterizing 3D — it does not accept a custom effect at all, so there is no promise/behaviour gap to catch. Every shader-based pass reports `isSupported() == false` and copies through: **399 pass · 92 skip · 0 fail**. |
| MOD-1631 | LLGL | 🟨 | **Measured:** `CustomEffects: yes` / `ExecutesShaderSourceEXT: no`, the `MOD-1699` pair, so every shader-based pass reports `isSupported() == false` and copies through. **399 pass · 89 skip · 0 fail** across the engine-layer suites, once three test-gating gaps this renderer exposed were closed: LLGL's validated OpenGL path **refuses a `RenderTargetCube` from the constructor**, so `CubeShadowMapTest.TheCubeIsAllocatedAndTheLightRoundTrips`, `SpotShadowMapTest.AnUnsupportedRendererIsReportedRatherThanFailing` and `MOD-1714`'s all-subsystems fixture failed rather than skipping. The first two now gate on `CNA_SKIP_WITHOUT_CUBE_RENDER_TARGETS`; the third holds its `CubeShadowMap` by pointer so the rest of the sweep still runs. **Recorded, not fixed:** a *second* `GraphicsDevice` on LLGL leaves LLGL's global state broken and the ensuing teardown throws `"in 'Get': expression 'current_' must not be null"` from an unwind path, which is `std::terminate`. `~LlglRenderer` is now `try`/`catch(...)`-wrapped — a destructor must not throw, independent of this — but the terminate persists from elsewhere in the failed-construction path, so `MultiDeviceTest` is excluded from LLGL's measurement rather than run. That belongs to `plan_llgl.md`. |
| MOD-1632 | Sokol | 🟨 | **Measured, and it found a process abort.** SOKOL answers `CustomEffects: yes` / `ExecutesShaderSourceEXT: no` — the `MOD-1699` pair again — so every shader-based pass reports `isSupported() == false` and copies through: **403 pass · 88 skip · 0 fail**. What the run first did instead was **abort the whole test process**: sokol_gfx keeps its state in one process-wide context and `sg_setup()` asserts `!_sg.valid`, so `MOD-1715`'s second-device probe killed `CnaTests` outright. Fixed in `Sokol::CreateGraphicsRenderer` — refuse by name, exactly as TinyGL does. The placement is the interesting part: the guard cannot live in `SetupSokol()`, because `SokolRenderer`'s initializer list creates a platform GL context first, and that makes the *new* context current, so the existing renderer's sokol objects are then torn down against the wrong context and sokol asserts again on the way out (`_sg_gl_discard_shader: glGetError() == 0`). Refusing before anything is constructed is the only point at which nothing has been disturbed. Under `NDEBUG` the assert is not there at all, which makes the same situation silent corruption rather than a crash. |
| MOD-1633 | Metal | ⛔ | **macOS only, by a hard configure gate.** `-DCNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERER=METAL` fails immediately on Linux: *"METAL renderer is currently supported only on macOS; iOS and tvOS remain unvalidated."* There is no cross-compilation route — Metal needs the macOS SDK and a Metal device — so this is refused with the reason rather than left open as if untried. |
| MOD-1634 | FNA3D | 🟨 | **Builds, cannot run here.** The FNA3D renderer compiles and links against the vendored FNA3D, but `FNA3D_CreateDevice` fails in this container for every driver — *"no FNA3D driver (SDL_GPU, Direct3D 11 or OpenGL) could create a device for this window"* — and forcing one with `FNA3D_FORCE_DRIVER=OpenGL` or `=SDL_GPU` changes nothing. With no device, every one of the 493 filtered tests fails at construction rather than reporting a capability, so there is nothing to measure. Same shape as `OPENGLES1`: an environment limitation stated rather than a CNA finding. |
| MOD-1635 | Wicked | ⛔ | **Blocked on an external checkout this container does not have.** `-DCNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERER=WICKED` fails at configure: `CNA_WICKED_ROOT` is empty and `CNA_WICKED_AUTO_FETCH=OFF`, so it wants a Wicked Engine clone pinned at `27c0df16` supplied by hand. Cloning and building a whole second engine to measure four capability answers is not proportionate to the row; the blocker and the exact recipe are recorded instead. |
| MOD-1637 | IGL | 🟨 | **Capabilities measured; the suite cannot complete, and the reason is IGL's.** It configures only with `-DENABLE_OPT=0` (its vendored glslang wants SPIRV-Tools that are not fetched here), and then reports `ThreeD: yes`, `CustomEffects: yes`, `ExecutesShaderSourceEXT: **no**` — the `MOD-1699` pair — with no float targets, no compute, no shadow sampling and no IBL. It is also, with Magnum, one of only two renderers measured that answer `MultiStreamVertexInput: yes`. The engine-layer run does not finish: in Debug, IGL's own soft assert *"Dangling IContext reference left behind"* raises `SIGTRAP` whenever a `GraphicsDevice` is destroyed; in Release the assert is compiled out and the same situation **segfaults**, reproducibly, in `DeviceLossTest` and again in `EffectPassTest.APassWithNoEffectIsNotSupportedAndCopiesInstead` — the *copy-through* path, which is what every unsupported pass takes. So the fallback this row wants to verify is exactly the path that crashes there. Both belong to `plan_igl.md`; the debug assert was right, and Release only hides it. |
| MOD-1636 | Cross-renderer pixel-parity test for the tonemap pass | 🟨 | **Blocked by having exactly one renderer that executes shader source.** A parity test compares the same input across two renderers that both really run the pass. After measuring **fourteen** renderers (`MOD-1600`…`MOD-1695`), EasyGL is still the only one that answers `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT() == true`: SDL_GPU, OpenGL2, OpenGL4, Sokol, Magnum, bgfx, LLGL, IGL and WebGPU all accept a custom effect and do not run its source, and SDL_Renderer, PortableGL, TinyGL, OpenGL1, Blend2D, OpenVG and Diligent do not accept one at all. A "parity" test across one implementation is a rename of that implementation's own test. Left open with the precondition named — it becomes writable the moment a second renderer executes shader source, which is what the rest of Phase 16 is for. |

### 16.6 Documented non-support (2D-only / non-shader renderers)

| ID | Renderers | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| MOD-1690 | SDL_Renderer | ✅ | **Measured on a real SDL_RENDERER build** (`cmake-build-sdlrenderer`). `cna_test_cnaext_caps` reports **no** to every one of the layer's capabilities and queries — `ThreeD`, `CustomEffects`, float and half-float targets, compute, instancing, multi-stream, `ExecutesShaderSourceEXT`, `SupportsShadowSamplingEXT`, `SupportsImageBasedLightingEXT` — which is the honest answer for a 2D-only renderer and means every pass reports `isSupported() == false` and copies through. The engine-layer suites end at **380 pass · 111 skip · 0 fail**. Getting there needed two **test-gating** fixes, both the same shape and both real: `TrianglePart`'s fixture built a `VertexBuffer` in `SetUp()`, which a 2D-only renderer refuses outright (`"SDL_Renderer does not support 3D: CreateVertexBuffer"`), so seven engine-layer tests *failed* rather than skipping; and `GltfMaterialToPbrMaterialTest.TheMaterialDetourReproducesTheImporterDrawParameters` imported a model for the same reason. Both now gate on `GraphicsCapability::ThreeD`. Seven failures that were really one documented boundary is exactly the noise that makes a per-renderer sweep unreadable. |
| MOD-1691 | Canvas, HTML_DOM, SVG_DOM, PixiJs | 🟨 | **Three of four built for real; PixiJS cannot configure at all.** With emsdk 6.0.7 (installed for `MOD-1717`), `libcna_graphics_ext.a` compiles for `CANVAS`, `HTML_DOM` and `SVG_DOM` — so the layer exists on the web DOM identities and, being renderer-agnostic above `IGraphicsRenderer`, constructs and passes through there as it does anywhere. Their capability answers cannot be *run* here: these renderers need a DOM, and neither node nor a headless probe can give them one, so the support matrix carries their declared answers rather than measured ones. **`PIXIJS` is a different story and it is a finding:** `-DCNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERER=PIXIJS` fails at configure — `cmake/RendererRegistry.cmake` has no namespace mapping for the identity, though `modules/renderers/pixijs/src/` implements `CNA::Internal::Renderers::PixiJs`. So the identity cannot be selected at all, which matches `plan_pixijs.md`'s own statement that it has never been built on a real toolchain; registering it is `plan_pixijs.md`'s `PIXIJS-84`, not an engine-layer change. Its `pixi.min.js` download is also blocked here, though that one is solvable: `npm pack pixi.js@7.4.2` yields `dist/pixi.min.js` whose SHA256 matches `CNA_PIXIJS_SHA256` exactly, so `-DCNA_PIXIJS_ROOT=` that path is a verified offline source. |
| MOD-1692 | Software, PortableGL | ✅ | **Both measured now.** Running the whole suite with `CNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERER=SOFTWARE` in `cmake-build-multi` found four `SkyboxRenderTest` failures that were not capability gaps but a wrong *promise*: SOFTWARE reports `CustomEffects`, accepts the sky shader, and then rasters with its own fixed path, so the sky came out the placeholder texture's white while `Skybox::isSupported()` said yes. Fixed by giving `Skybox` and all four shadow casters the two-part question `MOD-1699` established for `PostProcessPass` (`CustomEffects` **and** `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT`). After that the engine layer has **no** failures under SOFTWARE; the 13 remaining are XNA-layer and selection tests that assert the *build's default* renderer and so cannot pass when a non-default one is forced by environment variable. **PortableGL is now measured too** (`cmake-build-portablegl`): it reports `ThreeD: yes` and **no** to everything else the layer asks — `CustomEffects`, float and half-float targets, compute, instancing, multi-stream, and all three `…EXT` queries. So it rasters 3D and runs none of this layer's shaders, which is exactly what a CPU OpenGL renderer with no shader-source path should say. Engine-layer suites: **348 pass · 143 skip · 0 fail**, and the run takes 314 ms rather than the 22 s SDL_Renderer needs, because almost everything gates out before touching the rasterizer. |
| MOD-1693 | Skia, Blend2D, Direct2D, OpenVG | 🟨 | **Blend2D and OpenVG measured; Skia needs its pinned artifact, Direct2D needs Windows.** Both measured identities answer **no** to every capability and query the layer asks about, including `ThreeD` — they are 2D vector rasterizers, and the honest answer is that none of this runs there. Blend2D: **380 pass · 111 skip · 0 fail**. OpenVG: **334 pass · 157 skip · 0 fail** (and needs `libglu1-mesa-dev`, because ShivaVG calls `gluScaleImage`/`gluOrtho2D`). **OpenVG found a test that asserted something no renderer had contradicted yet:** `RequireCapabilityTest.ASupportedCapabilityReturnsWithoutThrowing` swept every capability, checked the quiet path on each supported one, and then `EXPECT_GT(checked, 0)` — on the assumption that every renderer supports *something*. OpenVG supports nothing at all, which makes the quiet path unreachable rather than untested; the test is about the helper, not the renderer, so it now skips and says so. |
| MOD-1694 | GDI, Glide, FreeDirect | 🟨 | **All three refuse to configure here, each for its own stated reason, and two of them are now reachable in principle.** GDI and Glide fail with *"only builds when targeting Windows"* — and the MinGW-w64 cross-build chain those messages point at is **proven working as of `MOD-1604`**, which built and ran `CnaTests.exe` under Wine, so these are a build away rather than a blocker. FreeDirect is different: it needs a sibling `free-direct` checkout next to this repo, which does not exist here. Not built for want of time rather than for want of a route, and said plainly. |
| MOD-1695 | DirectX 1–8, OpenGL1, OpenGLES1, TinyGL | 🟨 | **TinyGL and OpenGL1 measured; OpenGLES1 blocked by this container; DirectX 1–8 need Windows.** Both measurable identities answer the same way — `ThreeD: yes`, and **no** to `CustomEffects`, float and half-float targets, compute, instancing, multi-stream and all three `…EXT` queries — so every shader-based subsystem reports `isSupported() == false` and copies through. TinyGL: **348 pass · 143 skip · 0 fail**. OpenGL1: **402 pass · 89 skip · 0 fail**. Two things came out of it. **`OPENGL1` did not compile at all**: `OpenGL1RendererDescriptor.cpp` carried two unmatched braces from the merge in `2f00c201`, present on `next`, so the identity had never been built — fixed. And **TinyGL refuses a second `GraphicsDevice`** by design (it keeps its context in one process-wide global with no make-current entry point), which failed `MOD-1715`'s three `MultiDeviceTest` cases; they now gate on a new `SupportsASecondDevice()` probe in `EngineTestSupport.hpp` — probed by doing, like the other five, because the limit belongs to the native library rather than to anything CNA declares. **OpenGLES1 cannot be measured here:** the container's GLX advertises only `OpenGL ES 3.2` as its ES profile, so an ES 1.x context request fails with `BadAlloc` before any CNA code runs. DirectX 1–8 need Wine+DXVK and a Windows SDL3, which this container does not have. |
| MOD-1696 | Headless | ✅ | **Measured, and it corrected a documented claim.** The full suite under the default HEADLESS renderer had 21 engine-layer failures, from three limits Headless never advertises as capabilities: `Texture2D::GetData` refuses to read a render target back; `TextureCube::SetData` stores nothing; and a cube-face bind is recorded without becoming the current target, so the face-sized viewport that follows is rejected. All three are now *probed* by `modules/graphics-ext/tests/CNA/Graphics/EngineTestSupport.hpp` rather than assumed, and the affected tests SKIP with the reason. The cube-face probe deliberately uses a face larger than the back buffer, because a small one is accepted by a bind that does nothing. Consequence for the docs: the IBL precompute does **not** "work on every renderer including Headless" — the arithmetic is CPU-side, but it still has to write into a `TextureCube`, which Headless refuses; `docs/cnaext-engine-layer.md` and `NEXT_modern.md` are corrected. Result: 7814 run, 7419 passed, 395 skipped, **0 failed**. |
| MOD-1697 | Stub | ✅ | **Measured.** Stub refuses `SetRenderTargets` for a `RenderTarget2D` outright — a stronger limit than Headless's, and one the engine layer hits before it renders anything: `RenderPipeline`, all four shadow maps and the robustness suite all failed on it. Added a fourth probe, `CanBindRenderTargets`, kept separate from `CanReadRenderTargets` precisely because these two renderers fail at different points. 25 engine-layer tests now SKIP with the reason and **no engine-layer test fails under Stub**. The 29 remaining failures are outside this layer: 17 assert the build's default renderer (unavoidable when a non-default one is forced by environment variable) and 12 are XNA-layer `TextureCube`/content tests that need real cube storage. |
| MOD-1699 | Capability queries so a renderer's *promise* is askable, not inferred | ✅ | Added while measuring the Vulkan rollout, and not optional in hindsight. `GraphicsDevice::SupportsShadowSamplingEXT()` and `SupportsImageBasedLightingEXT()` (renderer virtuals, false by default, true on EasyGL) answer the question `GraphicsCapability::CustomEffects` cannot: whether the state an effect *accepts* everywhere is actually *used* here. Without them the shadow example did not fail on Vulkan, it **crashed** — its caster effect silently failed to compile and the draw went ahead with no effect applied. With them, four examples SKIP with a reason instead. **Extended while measuring `MOD-1692`:** the same distinction has a third form, `ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT()` — "will you actually run this source?" — which SOFTWARE and HEADLESS answer false. Every shader-based engine subsystem now asks the two-part question, not just `PostProcessPass`: `Skybox` and all four shadow casters (`ShadowMap`, `CascadedShadowMap`, `SpotShadowMap`, `CubeShadowMap`). Before the sweep, SOFTWARE reported a supported sky and rendered a blank white frame. |
| MOD-1698 | Support-matrix completeness check | ✅ | `scripts/check_cnaext_matrix.py` derives the identity list from `CNA::GraphicsRendererType` itself and fails if any identity is missing from the "Every renderer identity" table in `docs/cnaext-engine-layer.md` or carries no status. Registered as the ctest `CNAEXT_MatrixCompleteness`, so a renderer added later cannot quietly read as "fine". |

## Phase 17 — Tests, golden images, CI (`MOD-1700`–`MOD-1741`)

Cross-cutting verification that does not belong to a single subsystem.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| MOD-1700 | Test fixture: an offscreen `GraphicsDevice` usable without a display for engine-layer unit tests | ✅ | **Nothing new was needed, which is the finding.** The repo's existing convention — a default-constructed `GraphicsDevice` in a gtest fixture, run from the repo root with a real display (`Xvfb :99`) — is exactly this fixture, and every engine-layer suite in this plan uses it. Documented in `NEXT_modern.md` §3 (the two run conditions that matter) and in `docs/cnaext-perf.md`'s recipe. Inventing a second fixture beside it would have given the layer a test path nothing else in the repository uses. |
| MOD-1701 | Fake `IGraphicsRenderer` for pure-logic tests (counts draws, target switches, allocations) | ⛔ | **Not written, because it would have no consumer.** `IGraphicsRenderer` has 55 pure virtuals; a fake implementing them exists to serve assertions, and the assertions it was meant to serve are already made against the real renderer through `RenderPipeline::getGpuMemoryEstimateBytes()` (allocation), `getLastFramePassCount()` (pass count) and `RecordingPass` (ordering) — see `MOD-1713`'s hundred-resize test and `MOD-208`'s ordering tests. Adding 55 stub methods with nothing left to prove is the dead code `MOD-1901` exists to remove. |
| MOD-1702 | Fake `PostProcessPass` (records order, forces throw) | ✅ | `RecordingPass` in `PostProcessChainTests.cpp`: records its name and its source/destination pointers, which is what the ordering and ping-pong assertions need. **Deviation:** no throw-forcing variant — `MOD-208`'s exception behaviour is asserted against the built-in passes and the pipeline's own frame-state errors instead, so a pass that exists only to throw would be a second way of testing the same thing. |
| MOD-1703 | Golden-image harness for the engine layer (naming, tolerance, regeneration command) | ⛔ | No golden harness, because this plan has no goldens: every row that proposed one (`MOD-852`, `MOD-912`, `MOD-1009`, `MOD-1112`, `MOD-1242`, `MOD-1243`, `MOD-1244`, `MOD-1313`) was met by a measured property instead, each with its reason recorded in the row. A harness for a category with no members is infrastructure nobody runs. |
| MOD-1704 | Deterministic test scene definition (fixed camera, meshes, lights, materials) shared by all goldens | ⛔ | Same reason. The deterministic scene it would have shared is instead each example's own scene, built to make one property measurable — a shadow test's scene is a caster and a floor because that is what makes a shadow's *area* countable, and it would be a worse skybox test. |
| MOD-1705 | Float-image comparison utility (per-channel epsilon, HDR-aware) | ⛔ | Same reason: no HDR golden exists to compare. The float comparisons this plan does make are numeric and local — luminance sums, per-channel bounds, worst-case differences — and each is stated in the assertion that uses it. |
| MOD-1706 | Tolerance policy documented per subsystem (why SSAO's is looser than tonemap's) | ✅ | **Reinterpreted, and satisfied.** With no goldens there are no image tolerances to drift; what does need explaining is why each measured property's bound is what it is, and that is written where the bound is — the 1/255 on an 8-bit round trip, the 0.01 on a GPU-versus-CPU float integration, the `EXPECT_EQ` on a culled frame that must be *identical*. `docs/cnaext-perf.md` carries the same discipline for measurements. |
| MOD-1707 | CTest labels: `CnaExt`, `CnaExtDisplay`, `CnaExtGolden` | ✅ | `CnaExt` and `CnaExtDisplay` labels on every engine-layer ctest (`ctest -L CnaExt` runs all nine, `-L CnaExtDisplay` the seven that need a device). **`CnaExtGolden` is not created**: this plan committed to measured properties instead of golden images everywhere it could (`MOD-1242`, `MOD-1313`, `MOD-852`, …), so a label for a category with no members would be decoration. |
| MOD-1708 | ASan/LSan run of the whole engine-layer suite | ✅ — and it caught a test I had broken | `cmake-build-asan` (`-DCNA_SANITIZE=address -DCNA_CNAEXT=ON`), whole engine-layer suite: **no leak from CNA code**, the only report being the same external `libdbus`/`SDL_DBus_Init` residual. It also found `RenderPipelineSettingsTest.OutOfRangeValuesAreStoredRatherThanRejected` failing — a **pre-existing test that `MOD-730` had invalidated and I had not re-run the full suite to notice**. Reconciled rather than deleted: it is now `ExtremeButMeaningfulValuesAreStoredRatherThanRejected` (a threshold of 100 still goes through untouched, which is what `MOD-22` was really about) with a sibling asserting the half `MOD-730` narrowed, so the two rules are read together instead of looking like a contradiction. **Note for whoever runs this next:** the container restarts here kill detached background builds, so the ASan build has to be driven in bounded foreground chunks — Ninja resumes where it stopped. |
| MOD-1709 | A `CNA_CNAEXT=OFF` regression suite run (the layer must not affect anything) | ✅ | Run: `cmake-build-debug` (`CNA_CNAEXT=OFF`), full `CnaTests` from the repo root under Xvfb — **7556 ran, 7492 passed, 64 skipped, 0 failed** after one real regression this run caught and fixed. `plan_modern.md`'s own `MOD-1309` rows had added two engine-layer suites to a glTF conformance rung unconditionally, and the ladder checks both directions, so with the layer compiled out the rung named suites that did not exist. They now join the Draco pair's existing exclusion mechanism, which verifies their source presence instead. That is precisely what this row is for: a regression invisible in the build the work was done in. |
| MOD-1710 | Baseline capture: record today's full-suite result before Phase 1 lands | ✅ | Done. 2026-08-17, `cmake-build-cnaext`, full `CnaTests` from the repo root under Xvfb: **6360 ran, 6351 pass, 8 skip, 1 fail** (an X11-connection hiccup in `EffectApplyTest.DisposeIsIdempotentAndDoesNotThrow`, passes standalone). Recorded in `NEXT_modern.md` §3 with the two run conditions that matter (repo-root CWD, a real display). |
| MOD-1711 | Per-phase regression gate: full suite re-run at the end of each phase | ✅ | Done for every phase this plan has completed: `NEXT_modern.md` §3 carries one row per phase boundary, from the pre-plan baseline through Phase 15. Each is a full `CnaTests` run from the repo root under Xvfb, and the row records ran/passed/skipped/failed rather than a verdict. |
| MOD-1712 | Performance-tracking file (`docs/cnaext-perf.md`) with a stable measurement recipe | ✅ | `docs/cnaext-perf.md`, opening with the recipe — machine, build, method, reproduce command — because a number without one is not a measurement. Every perf row of this plan is collected there in one format, including the caveat that these are software-rasteriser numbers meaningful against each other rather than predictions for any GPU. |
| MOD-1713 | Fuzz/robustness: absurd sizes (0×0, 1×1, 8192×8192), rapid resize, rapid setting flips | ✅ | `EngineLayerRobustnessTests.cpp`: 1×1, 1×720, 1280×1, 17×23 and 2048×2048 frames all render; zero and negative sizes are *refused* rather than clamped, so a caller's arithmetic bug surfaces immediately; a hundred consecutive resizes return to exactly the baseline memory estimate; every setting is flipped across 64 frames in every combination; 200 frames leave the pass count and memory unchanged; and the two frame-state errors (double `begin`, unmatched `end`) throw. |
| MOD-1714 | Device-loss test across the whole layer | ✅ | `DeviceLifecycleTests.cpp`: pipeline + all four shadow maps + prepass + skybox + bloom/SSAO/FXAA/tonemap alive together, `DeviceReset` raised, every subsystem renders again. Deviation: raising `GraphicsDevice::DeviceReset` is the layer's actual loss signal -- there is no `DebugSimulateContextLoss()` in this codebase, and a real GL context loss cannot be provoked under llvmpipe. 20 repeated resets must not grow `getStatistics().renderTargetBytes` past the first frame's, and a reset arriving between `begin()` and `end()` is ignored rather than obeyed (pins the `frameOpen_` guard against dead-code removal). |
| MOD-1715 | Multi-window / multi-device sanity (two pipelines, two devices) | ✅ | `MultiDeviceTest`: two devices each with their own pipeline and settings -- no shared state, a reset on one leaves the other's targets untouched, and destroying one pipeline leaves its sibling renderable. The layer holds no process-global device state. |
| MOD-1716 | 64-bit/32-bit and Debug/Release build sanity for the layer | 🟨 | **Debug/Release measured; 32-bit not buildable here.** `cmake-build-cnaext-release` (`-DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release -DCNA_CNAEXT=ON`) configures, builds and runs **7940 · 7876 pass · 64 skip · 0 fail** -- identical to Debug, including the skip list compared name by name, and 22% faster (230 s vs 294 s). That equality is what the row is for: an optimised build is where a tolerance-based image assertion or an uninitialised read would change its verdict, and none does. 32-bit is refused with a reason: no i386 multilib in this container (`g++ -m32` cannot find `Scrt1.o`; `/usr/lib/i386-linux-gnu` does not exist) and no 32-bit SDL3/GL/FFmpeg, so the build fails at the C runtime rather than in CNA. See `NEXT_modern.md` §3. |
| MOD-1717 | Emscripten build check for the engine layer (WEBGL2 profile) | ✅ | It builds **and runs**. `cmake-build-web-cnaext` (emsdk 6.0.7, `-DCNA_CNAEXT=ON -DCNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERER=WEBGL2`, Release) compiles `libcna_graphics_ext.a`, links an engine-layer example, and `node cna_example_cnaext_settings.js` prints `=== All PASS ===` -- so the layer's CPU-side behaviour is exercised on a 32-bit wasm ABI with libc++, which is the nearest thing available to the 32-bit half `MOD-1716` could not do. Three things had to be dealt with, only one of them ours: **(1) fixed** -- `-lembind` was missing, because `GraphicsRendererSelectionEmscripten.cpp` uses `emscripten::val` and Emscripten does not link `libembind` implicitly; *every* Emscripten executable in this repository failed at `wasm-ld` with undefined `_emval_*`, and `cna_core` now carries the option as an `INTERFACE` link option. **(2)** vendored Draco's `draco_emscripten.cmake` requires the `EMSCRIPTEN` environment variable by name, so `export EMSCRIPTEN="$EMSDK/upstream/emscripten"` is needed before `emcmake`. **(3) recorded, not patched** -- vendored Draco 1.5.7's `src/draco/io/ply_reader.cc` calls `std::all_of` without `<algorithm>`, which libstdc++ tolerates and libc++ does not; it is a pinned submodule, and the web build uses the repository's existing `-DCNA_ENABLE_DRACO=OFF` mode. See `NEXT_modern.md` §3. |
| MOD-1718 | Android build check (GLES3 profile) | ⛔ | **Blocked by this environment's network policy, not by the code.** The Android NDK is distributed only by Google: the agent proxy answers `403` to `CONNECT dl.google.com:443` (its own `recentRelayFailures` records the denial), and the Ubuntu `google-android-ndk-*-installer` packages are 16 KB shims that download from the same host -- their control scripts name `https://dl.google.com` with `mirrors.neusoft.edu.cn` as the only alternative, and that mirror plus `mirrors.cloud.tencent.com` are equally unreachable from here. Without a sysroot there is no bionic, no `libGLESv3`, and nothing an `-DANDROID_ABI=…` configure could do but fail at the first header. Nothing about the engine layer was found to be Android-hostile: `MOD-1717` compiles and *runs* it against a GLES-shaped target (WebGL2/Emscripten), and `MOD-1719` compiles it for a second non-Linux ABI. The row is left refused with the blocker named rather than turned into a paper claim. |
| MOD-1719 | MinGW cross-compile check for the D3D renderers' engine-layer paths | ✅ | `mingw-cnaext-spike/check.sh`: all 39 `modules/graphics-ext/src/*.cpp` cross-compile under `x86_64-w64-mingw32-g++` with `-Wall -Wextra`, three times (`CNA_RENDERER_DIRECTX11`/`DIRECTX12`/`DIRECTX9`). The half that matters is `windows_header_collisions.cpp`: `<windows.h>` with no defensive defines, then every public engine-layer header -- `near` and `far` **are** live object-like macros there, so `begin(…, float near, float far)` would break every Windows build, and the probe `#error`s if those macros are absent so it cannot pass by measuring nothing (verified by temporarily adding a `float near` parameter). **Finding:** the MSVC `min`/`max` hazard cannot be reproduced by MinGW, which guards them with `#ifndef __cplusplus`; `msvc_minmax_collisions.cpp` reinstates them by hand and shows the engine layer is clean while **sharp-runtime** is not -- `SharpRuntimeHelper.hpp` writes `std::numeric_limits<T>::max()` unparenthesised (lines 155/160/165/170/175), which a real MSVC D3D build would fail on. That is another repository, so it is recorded rather than fixed here, and the script scores that probe on blast radius (nothing originating under `modules/graphics-ext`) instead of on success. Does not link: SDL3/GL/FFmpeg for Windows are not present in this container. |
| MOD-1740 | Wire `scripts/check_cnaext_guards.sh` (`MOD-3`) into the test suite | ✅ | `CNAEXT_GuardDiscipline` runs `scripts/check_cnaext_guards.sh` as a ctest — pure text, no device, no display, and it runs in **every** configuration including `CNA_CNAEXT=OFF`, where an unguarded file would otherwise not even be compiled. |
| MOD-1741 | Coverage report for `modules/graphics-ext/` | ✅ | Measured, not estimated: `docs/cnaext-coverage.md`, from a real `--coverage` build (`cmake-build-cnaext-coverage`, GCC 13, `-O0`) driven by the whole suite under Xvfb — **7940 · 7876 pass · 64 skip · 0 fail**, the same verdict as the ordinary Debug build. **Lines 94.2% (2822/2997), functions 97.8% (523/535), branches 57.5% (2218/3859)**, with a per-file table and the reproduction recipe. The branch figure is mostly GCC's implicit exception edges rather than untested logic, and the doc says which files are the exception. Five gaps are named, and the most useful finding is a measurement artefact worth knowing: `AsciiFontAtlas.cpp` reads as 39% because its two uncovered functions are proved by an **example program**, not a `CnaTests` case, and this measurement drives `CnaTests` only. The one real hole a test could close today is `BlitPass::getName()`/`isSupported()`, never called by anything (`MOD-1742`). The rest — `ScopedRenderTarget`'s two `catch` blocks, `InstancedRendererEXT`'s per-instance fallback, `ComputeShader`/`StorageBuffer`'s refusal paths — need a renderer that says no, not a new assertion. This is a separate artefact from `docs/coverage.md`, which is a static API-surface estimate for the whole project. |
| MOD-1742 | `BlitPass::getName()` and `isSupported()` have no test | ✅ | Two cases in `PostProcessChainTests.cpp`. `getName()` is pinned as `"Blit"` **and** as a reference into storage the pass owns (`&getName() == &getName()`), so a profiler keeping the label across frames is not left holding a dangling one. `isSupported()` is pinned as unconditionally true, which is the claim that matters: a plain copy needs no capability, so a chain can always fall back to it, and if that ever starts depending on the device the callers relying on it need revisiting. 5/5 `BlitPassTest` cases pass. |

## Phase 18 — Documentation, examples, demos (`MOD-1800`–`MOD-1833`)

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| MOD-1800 | `docs/cnaext-engine-layer.md` — complete capability boundary document | ✅ | `docs/cnaext-engine-layer.md` now covers every implemented subsystem — HDR pipeline, post-process passes, shadows (directional, cascaded, punctual), sky, IBL, materials, instancing/LOD/culling, compute and auto-exposure — each with its capability gate, its contract on the caller, and its measured cost. Written in `docs/webgpu-renderer.md`'s style: the limitations are in the same table as the features. |
| MOD-1801 | Per-renderer support matrix kept current at the end of every phase | ✅ | The per-renderer matrix in that document has a row for every subsystem this plan has landed, and was updated at each phase boundary rather than at the end. |
| MOD-1802 | "Getting started with the engine layer" tutorial (build flags → first HDR frame) | ✅ | `docs/cnaext-getting-started.md`: build flags, the two calls around an existing `Draw`, the deliberate intermediate state where nothing is enabled and the frame is unchanged, then the settings that turn on HDR and bloom — and a table of what to add next with what each *costs* the caller, since several of them (SSAO's inputs, shadows' second draw) are contracts rather than switches. |
| MOD-1803 | Migration note for existing CNA games (nothing changes unless you opt in) | ✅ | The tutorial's §0, stated before any instruction and backed by two tests rather than by assertion: `RenderPipelineTest.AnInertPipelineNeverAllocatesASceneTarget` and the new `cnaext_bloom_test`, which renders the same scene with and without a pipeline and compares **all 16 384 pixels** — 0 differ. |
| MOD-1804 | Per-subsystem doc sections (HDR, post, shadows, sky/IBL, geometry, compute) | ✅ | Each subsystem's section was written as its phase completed — the reason the document is accurate rather than aspirational. |
| MOD-1805 | Shader-authoring guide for custom passes | ✅ | The tutorial's §5, three rules: the UV origin belongs to the renderer (a shader that hardcodes `1.0 - v` renders upside down on the next renderer), sample your input rather than the target you are writing, and declare the capability you need and copy through where it is absent rather than silently doing nothing. |
| MOD-1806 | `CNAEXT.md` kept in sync (statuses + corrections) | ✅ | `CNAEXT.md`'s N-numbers were flipped at each phase boundary with the correction inline (N30/N31/N32/N40 in Phases 8-11, N41-N43 in 12, N04/N52 in 13, N50/N51 in 14, N70/N71/N73 in 15), so the design document and the plan have not diverged at any point. |
| MOD-1807 | CLAUDE.md pointer to this plan and `docs/cnaext-engine-layer.md` | ✅ | A `## The CNAEXT Engine Layer` section in `CLAUDE.md` pointing at `CNAEXT.md`, `plan_modern.md`, `NEXT_modern.md`, `docs/cnaext-engine-layer.md` and `docs/cnaext-perf.md`, with the build directory and the default-off guarantee — the `plan_skia.md` precedent, so a future session does not re-derive this subsystem's state from its code. |
| MOD-1808 | Showcase demo: a small 3D scene using every subsystem at once | ✅ | `modules/graphics-ext/examples/cnaext_showcase_test.cpp`, registered as the `CNAEXT_Showcase` ctest, documented in `docs/cnaext-showcase.md`; **8/8 checks pass**. Ground plane, an emissive PBR box under an image-based light, 576 instanced cubes behind it with frustum culling and LOD, a sky and a sun — through prepass, SSAO, HDR, bloom, ACES and FXAA in one frame. Every check is an **A/B against the same frame with exactly one subsystem off**, which is the failure a per-subsystem test cannot see. Three things it caught while being written, all of them the caller's contract rather than the layer's: attaching the skybox inside the render path made the inert comparison A/B a frame *with* a sky against one without (all 16 384 pixels differed); the shadow's receiving half is the effect's `setShadowsEnabledEXT`, not the settings, so the first version measured 234.3 against 234.3; and the SSAO A/B first scored on pixels darkened by ≥2, where the count is 14 140 of 16 384 — a check that would have passed just as happily if the pass had become a global dimmer, now scored on ≥8 with an upper bound. The shadow check counts over the whole frame rather than a guessed window, with both a floor and a ceiling, for the same reason. |
| MOD-1809 | Demo: 2D `SpriteBatch` game gaining HDR bloom in ~10 lines | ✅ | `modules/graphics-ext/examples/cnaext_bloom_test.cpp`, registered as ctest `CNAEXT_BloomInTenLines`; 4/4 checks. The engine-layer part really is ten lines, marked as such in the source. It also asserts the inert case pixel-for-pixel and that a higher intensity spreads more light (674 132 → 1 053 284 units of glow outside the sprite). |
| MOD-1810 | Demo: glTF character with PBR + IBL + shadows | 🟨 | **The join is proved; the "character" is not, and cannot be here.** `modules/graphics-ext/examples/cnaext_gltf_pbr_test.cpp` (`CNAEXT_GltfPbr` ctest, 5/5) checks the thing every other test in this plan assumes: that a material arriving through the **runtime importer** — `ContentManager::Load<Model>` on a `.gltf`, no offline step — answers to the engine layer the same way a hand-built `PbrEffect` does. It imports as a `PbrEffect`, an `ImageBasedLightEXT` bound to it changes what it renders (mean luma 13.5 → 29.4), it is really rasterized into the shadow map, the skinned fixture imports with `SkinningData` and a shadow-receiving effect, and an inert pipeline is still pixel-identical. The shadow check is deliberately not `didShadowPassRun()` alone — a caster callback that drew nothing would answer that the same way — so the map's 1 048 576 texels are compared against the map an **empty** callback produces. **Deviation:** there is no glTF character in this repository. `tests/assets/gltf/` is a 145-fixture *conformance* corpus whose largest mesh is six vertices; vendoring an art asset for one demo would add a licence and a megabyte for no verification the fixtures cannot give. What is unproven is a character rig, and the row stays 🟨 rather than ✅ for exactly that. |
| MOD-1811 | Screenshots/GIFs for the README's feature list | ✅ | Six 256×256 PNGs under `docs/images/cnaext/` (192 KB total), produced by `cna_test_cnaext_showcase --screenshots DIR` — the composed frame plus one per A/B variant, so the images are the *same frames the checks measured* rather than separately staged renders. `docs/cnaext-showcase.md` puts each next to what changes in it. No GIFs: nothing in the layer animates, and a GIF of a static frame would be a bigger file saying less. |
| MOD-1812 | `README.md` mention of the engine layer with its honest maturity label | ✅ | The README's feature section already described the layer; what it lacked was the label. Its heading now reads **"opt-in, experimental"** and it opens with the maturity statement in the same shape the `WEBGPU`/`DILIGENT`/`SKIA` entries use — implemented and tested, HDR spine end to end, **but the API is still moving**: engine revision 2 already carried renames, so pin a revision, read `CNA_CNAEXT_ENGINE_VERSION` and the changelog when you move. Points at `CNAEXT.md` §9.1 for which parts are settled and which are not, rather than restating it. |
| MOD-1813 | Nova-3D integration note (what Nova-3D can rely on today) | ✅ | `docs/cnaext-nova3d.md`, and the split is the point: **rely on it** (a table of the seven subsystems with where each one's contract lives, plus the two tested guarantees behind it — an inert pipeline is pixel-identical to no pipeline, and every subsystem survives a device reset), **ask before you rely on it** (the `MOD-1699` two-part question, and that `isSupported() == false` is normal rather than an error — an unsupported pass copies its input through so a chain stays correct), and **do not rely on it yet** (any renderer but EasyGL, the API's spelling, the ⛔ decisions, threading). `CNAEXT.md` §10 now points at it, distinguishing what that section *intends* from what is implemented and measured. |

## Phase 19 — API stabilization / Nova-3D readiness (`MOD-1900`–`MOD-1924`)

Runs only after Phases 1–13 are ✅. Its purpose is to stop the engine-layer API from drifting once a
real consumer exists.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| MOD-1900 | Full API review of `CNA::Graphics` against the naming/documentation rules | ✅ | The documentation half was already gated (`MOD-7`'s `CNAEXT_DoxygenGroup`); this row did the naming half and gated it too — `scripts/check_cnaext_naming.py`, the `CNAEXT_NamingRule` ctest. Six deviations from `MOD-6`'s lowerCamelCase rule were found and **renamed**: `detail::RequireCapability` → `requireCapability`, `detail::NameOfCapability` → `nameOfCapability`, `detail::ReportShaderCompileFailure` → `reportShaderCompileFailure`, `EngineException::NotSupported` → `notSupported`, and the private helpers `DepthEffect::EnsurePaletteTextures` → `ensurePaletteTextures` and `RenderPipeline::DrawSkybox` → `drawSkybox`. All 44 call sites are inside `modules/graphics-ext`, so nothing outside the module moved. Ten names are **kept** as named exceptions, each with its reason in the script: six `Effect` overrides (`GetTypeName`/`Clone`/`OnApply` × 2 — the base class chose those names), `PbrMaterial::GetHashCode`/`ToString` (deliberate `System.Object` mirrors), and `AsciiPostProcessEffect::Draw`/`GetLastGridDimensions`, which predate `MOD-6` **and are part of the C ABI surface** (`modules/c-api/src/CnaCApiGraphicsExt.cpp`) — that last one is the reason a rename there is not this layer's to make. The gate also fails on a *stale* exemption, so the list cannot outlive what it excuses. Full suite after the renames: **7942 · 7878 pass · 64 skip · 0 fail**; all 20 `CNAEXT_*` ctests pass. |
| MOD-1901 | Remove or rename anything that turned out to be dead (`RenderPipelineSettings` fields nothing reads) | ✅ — and the sweep found two real gaps | A script derived the 304 public member names from `modules/graphics-ext/include/CNA/Graphics/*.hpp` and looked for a use of each outside its own declaration and definition. **No dead setting**: every `RenderPipelineSettings` field has a production consumer, so nothing was removed. Four names came back unused and two were real. **`RenderPipeline::setDepthNormalInputs` had never been called** — by any test, example or caller in the repository — while being named as *the* route to feed SSAO in `docs/cnaext-engine-layer.md`, `docs/cnaext-getting-started.md` and `DepthNormalPrepass.hpp`. The path everything points at was the one path never run; `cnaext_ssao_test` now drives it (12/12), including clearing it back to null. **`CascadedShadowMap::getBlendBand` and `isDebugTintEnabled` had no reader** — their setters were used, so nothing had ever checked the setters store anything; the round trip is now asserted. The other two were false positives of the search (a constructor reached through `make_unique`). |
| MOD-1902 | Const-correctness and `[[nodiscard]]` sweep | ✅ | Swept, and then made repeatable: `scripts/check_cnaext_nodiscard.py`, wired as the `CNAEXT_AccessorConventions` ctest, checks **162 accessors across 52 headers** for two rules — every value-returning `get`/`is`/`has` is `[[nodiscard]]`, and is `const` when it takes no argument. Verified to fail on a planted canary for both rules. The sweep found the layer already consistent apart from **three pure functions in `CNA/Internal/Graphics/Ascii/AsciiFontAtlas.hpp`** (`BuildAsciiFontAtlasImageData`, `BuildAsciiFontAtlas`, `GetAsciiGlyphBitmap`), now `[[nodiscard]]`. Four exemptions are named in the script with their reason rather than left as silent holes: `getEngineLayerVersion`/`getEngineLayerVersionString` are free functions, and `getTargetPool`/`getSettings` are the non-const halves of const/non-const overload pairs. Two deliberate non-decisions recorded: `AutoExposureEXT::update` and `RenderPipelineSettings::applyFromStringEXT` return values that a caller may legitimately ignore — both change state as their primary job — so `[[nodiscard]]` would only force `(void)` casts at honest call sites. Also checked and clean: no by-value heavy parameters in any public header, and `LodGroupEXT::selectIndex`/`select` are correctly non-const because they carry the hysteresis state. |
| MOD-1903 | Ownership review (raw vs `unique_ptr` vs borrowed) documented per class | ✅ | `docs/cnaext-ownership.md`: every member of every public header read, and the answer is that the layer uses exactly **three** shapes — *owned* (`unique_ptr`, released in the destructor), *borrowed* (settable raw pointer, caller keeps it alive), *attached* (`GraphicsDevice&`, constructor-injected, never rebound). No `shared_ptr` anywhere, and no class deletes anything it did not create. Three classes accept either an object the caller owns or one they build, and all three spell it identically — a possibly-empty `unique_ptr` beside a raw pointer that names whichever is live (`EffectPass`, `Skybox`, `PostProcessChain`); the doc names that idiom so a fourth class copies it rather than inventing a variant. Two inconsistencies are recorded rather than papered over: `AsciiPostProcessEffect` keeps `GraphicsDevice*` where everything else keeps `GraphicsDevice&` (a leftover from before the layer's naming rules — its constructor takes the reference and immediately takes its address, so it is never null), and `PbrMaterial` borrows seven raw texture pointers because content-pipeline textures are shared between materials and owning them would need reference counting the layer does not use. The single most important fact, stated once: **nothing in `CNA::Graphics` may outlive its `GraphicsDevice`**, and nothing tries to detect it if it does. |
| MOD-1904 | Bump `CNA_CNAEXT_ENGINE_VERSION` to 2 and document what changed | ✅ | Bumped, and the number now has somewhere to point: `docs/cnaext-engine-changelog.md` says what each revision is. Revision 2 lists the **six renames** `MOD-1900` made (with old → new), the eight new types, the new members on existing types, and — the entry most likely to change what a caller sees — that `Skybox` and all four shadow casters now answer `isSupported()` with the two-part question from `MOD-1699`, so a renderer that accepts a shader without running it reports `false` rather than rendering a wrong frame. Three behaviour changes are called out beside the API ones (`end()` unbinds before the chain; both depth encoders clamp to `0.99999994`; the shadow casters no longer brick on a refused bind). The header now says the rule the row was really about: **bump on anything a consumer could notice, additions included**, because a consumer feature-testing on the revision needs the number to move when a feature arrives, not only when one breaks. `EngineLayerVersionTest` pins `2` and the string form as literals rather than re-deriving them from the macro — a test that re-derives the implementation measures nothing, and the manual edit is the intended cost of a bump. |
| MOD-1905 | Write the "engine layer v1 is stable" statement, or an honest "still moving" statement | ✅ | **Still moving** — `CNAEXT.md` §9.1, dated, with §9's "not an ABI guarantee" bullet now pointing at it instead of leaving "until it stabilizes" to the reader's optimism. The value is in the line being drawn rather than in the verdict: *settled* are the layer's shape (pipeline + pass chain + one shadow class per light type + skybox/IBL, which every subsystem landed against without fighting), the ownership rules, the `MOD-1699` two-part support question, and the naming/accessor conventions now that ctests hold them. *Still moving* are per-renderer behaviour (EasyGL is complete, the rest of Phase 16 is open, and a renderer picking up a subsystem has forced interface changes before), the set of `…EXT()` device queries (every renderer that promised something it did not do produced a new one — there is no reason the four that exist are the last), and compute/storage buffers, which have exactly one implementation. Ends with what it means for a consumer, Nova-3D included: pin a revision, read the changelog when you move, expect renames of the kind revision 2 already carried. |
| MOD-1906 | Final regression + perf sweep across every implemented renderer | ✅ | **Reference renderer: 7944 ran · 7880 pass · 64 skip · 0 fail**, exit 0. Every renderer measured is in one table in `NEXT_modern.md` §3 alongside it — twenty identities, plus `CNA_CNAEXT=OFF` and Release, and the reason each unmeasurable identity could not be run here. Two failures were seen on the way and neither was a regression, which is itself the finding worth keeping: the audio stress test failed its own anti-vacuity assertion under three-way machine load, and `TerminalRestoration.SighupGivesTheTerminalBack` failed because the run was started under **`nohup`**, which sets SIGHUP to `SIG_IGN` — a disposition `fork` preserves, so the child the test signals ignores it and exits 0 instead of dying. Confirmed by running that one test with and without `nohup`; the sweep was then re-run in the foreground for the number above. The perf half needs no new measurement and says so: `docs/cnaext-perf.md` holds every timing, all on EasyGL, because on every other renderer the passes copy through and a timing would measure the copy. |
| MOD-1907 | Retrospective: which CNAEXT.md design decisions did not survive contact | ✅ | `CNAEXT.md` §9.2, seven entries, written so a reader of §5 knows which paragraphs to distrust. The three that cost the most: **"ask the capability" was the wrong question** — `CustomEffects` means a renderer *accepts* a shader, not that it runs it, which produced passes reporting success while drawing nothing three separate times before the lesson stuck (`MOD-1699`); **four proposed capability enumerators became two**, because `StorageBuffers` was a synonym of `ComputeShaders` and `SeamlessCubeMapFilter` had nothing to ask about once the convolution ran on the CPU, and an enumerator that answers nothing invites a caller to branch on it; and **golden images were planned eight times and used zero times**, each replaced by a measured property, because a golden tells you a frame changed rather than that the thing you cared about is true. Also recorded: `CreateRenderTarget2DEx` was proposed for a virtual that already existed (the design was written against a mental model of the renderer interface, not the header); the two scope calls that were decided the same way and landed in opposite places (auto-exposure deferred with a named blocker and shipped once compute existed, SMAA/TAA declined for reasons that did not expire); and, on the other side, the two house rules and the layer's overall shape, which held exactly as written and are the part of §5 still worth trusting. |

---

## Phase 20 — Beyond the XNA-era pipeline (`MOD-2000`–`MOD-2099`)

Phases 0–19 built the pipeline an XNA-era game would recognise: forward shading, four post-process
passes, shadow maps, IBL from a static environment. This phase is the list of what a *modern*
renderer has that the layer does not — written down as tasks rather than left as an absence a reader
has to notice.

**Scope decision (owner, 2026-08-19): EasyGL only.** Every row below is EasyGL-first and there is
deliberately **no per-renderer rollout section**. Phase 16 measured twenty renderers and found that
none but EasyGL executes this layer's shader source, so a "rollout" row for any other renderer would
be a row waiting on that renderer's own plan, not on this one. When a second renderer runs GLSL,
this phase gets its 16-style matrix; until then it would be twenty columns of the same sentence.

**What "EasyGL only" costs, stated once.** The reference profile is GL ES 3.0 (compute at ES 3.1,
image bindings desktop-GL only — `MOD-1514`) and desktop GL 3.3. Several items below are refused
against *that* profile rather than in principle; each says which, so the refusal expires if the
profile does.

### 20.1 Screen-space reflections (`MOD-2000`–`MOD-2009`)

The prepass this needs already exists and already produces exactly the right things:
`DepthNormalPrepass` writes **linear view-space depth** and **view-space normals**, and
`PostProcessContext` already carries `sourceDepth`, `sourceNormals`, `projection`,
`inverseProjection`, `nearPlane` and `farPlane`. SSR is the largest modern feature reachable without
new infrastructure.

| ID | Task | Status | Acceptance criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| MOD-2000 | `SsrPass`: view-space ray march against the prepass depth buffer | ✅ | **Done and verified on EasyGL: 16 tests.** A tilted plane reflects a nearer band above it; a plane alone reflects nothing; a camera-facing surface, a zero depth and the cleared far plane reflect nothing; missing prepass inputs copy through pixel-for-pixel. **Four findings, none of them about SSR.** **(1) Sampling a render target is vertically flipped** relative to sampling a `Texture2D` holding the same rows -- but *not* when the target was filled by rasterising geometry into it, where the same flip applies on write and cancels. `MOD-2009` measured that; the tests here build their images with a blit, which flips once, and account for it in one place. **(2) The per-unit sampler state does not reach a texture bound through `ShaderEffect::SetTexture`** -- measured, `PointClamp` and `LinearClamp` return the same filtered value. Depth must be point-sampled (filtering a depth edge invents a surface at a distance nothing is at, and the march hits it one texel early, in front of the object it was looking for), so the pass snaps the coordinate to the texel centre in the shader. The sampler-state route was written first, measured, and removed rather than left in looking correct. **(3) The `depthBias` was not in the plan and is not optional:** the first step of a ray leaving a surface is still level with it, the difference is a few ULPs either side of zero, and half of those are positive -- every mirror reflects its own colour, which looks like a working reflection until you notice it never shows anything else. **(4) "Nothing here" has two spellings.** The prepass clears depth to **white**, so an empty pixel is 1.0, and this pass tested only for zero: the sky became a surface at the camera with a reflection marched out of it. Found by `MOD-2009` and fixed here; both spellings are now tested. |
| MOD-2001 | Binary-search refinement of the coarse hit | ✅ | The march ends holding a bracket -- the last point in front of the surface and the first behind it -- and six bisections close it. Six because that takes a step to 1/64 of itself, below one texel for every step count the pass accepts; past that the snapping to texel centres decides the answer and further halving changes nothing. Asserted as the thing a viewer would notice rather than as a distance: **the answer stops depending on the step count.** Eight steps and sixty-four now agree; with the refinement removed the same test reads **215 and 250**, which is a coarse march overshooting the edge it crossed -- reflected edges stair-step, and halving the step moves the whole reflection instead of sharpening it. Verified failing with `kCnaRefineSteps = 0` before being left passing. |
| MOD-2002 | Rejection: backfaces, thickness, and screen-edge fade | ✅ | Three rejections, three tests, each differing from the passing hit test by exactly one variable. **Backfaces:** a surface whose normal points the way the ray is travelling is one the ray came up behind, and reflecting its front-facing colour puts the far side of an object into a mirror that cannot see it -- a wrong image that looks entirely plausible. Adding the check broke the existing hit test, correctly: its band shared the floor's normal, so the two were parallel and the ray did arrive at the band's back. The scene now turns the band to face what reflects off it, which is what an object standing in front of a floor actually does. **Thickness:** the upper tolerance and the counterpart of the depth bias -- a thickness smaller than the gap means the ray flew past the band rather than into it. **Edge fade:** `edgeFade`, a screen fraction clamped to [0, 0.5], smoothstepped from the border; nothing outside the viewport was ever drawn, so a reflection ending near it is about to reflect information the frame does not have. Asserted as a comparison -- the same reflection with the fade band wide enough to reach it comes back measurably weaker. Without it a reflection stops along a hard line down the edge of the screen, which is the usual giveaway of the technique. |
| MOD-2003 | Roughness-aware reflection blur | ✅ | **The blocker was where to put roughness, not how to blur.** It lives in the material, the prepass writes geometry, and `PostProcessContext` carries neither. Decided: **the alpha of the normal target**, which carried a constant 1.0 and was read by nobody. A third render target was the alternative and is worse -- MRT is capped, this pass already falls back to two passes without it, and a third output would make that fallback three passes for one scalar. `DepthNormalPrepass::setRoughness` can be changed between draws inside an open pass, which is the only way a scene with more than one material can describe itself. **The default is 0 -- a mirror -- not glTF's fully-rough 1**, so an app that never calls it gets the sharp reflection it had before roughness existed rather than a silently blurred frame it cannot explain. The spread itself is four taps around the hit widened by roughness, deliberately *not* snapped to texel centres: filtering is the point here, where for the depth comparison it was the bug (`MOD-2000`). Not the BRDF integral a real cone asks for, and the row does not claim it is -- it is the difference between a mirror and a brushed floor, which is what the roughness describes. Tested as the same scene twice with nothing changed but that alpha. |
| MOD-2004 | `RenderPipelineSettings`: SSR enable, max distance, step count, thickness, intensity | ✅ | Seven fields -- the five the row asked for plus `SSRDepthBias` and `SSREdgeFade`, which `MOD-2000` and `MOD-2002` turned out to need. Off by default, so an existing pipeline renders identically. The pass reads the bag in preference to its own defaults, matching every other pass: a pipeline that applied a quality preset must not be overruled by a pass-local default nobody set. Distances clamp at zero and the edge fade at half the frame, because a settings bag restored from a file should not throw over a value whose out-of-range case has an obvious meaning. |
| MOD-2005 | Pipeline placement and ordering | ✅ | **Between SSAO and bloom**, and both neighbours are decisions: after SSAO because what a mirror shows should be the shaded scene rather than the unshaded one, and before the tonemapper for the reason bloom is -- a reflection carries scene-referred colour, and mixing it in after the range has been compressed makes a reflected highlight indistinguishable from a reflected white wall. Enabling it also takes the pipeline out of its inert short circuit, like every other pass. **The pipeline gained a camera, which it has never had.** `setSkyboxCamera` told the sky a view and a projection; nothing else needed one, so `PostProcessContext`'s `projection`, `inverseProjection`, `nearPlane` and `farPlane` were declared and never filled. `setCamera(view, projection, near, far)` fills them and sets the skybox camera too, so a game that draws a sky and runs reflections supplies one camera rather than two that can drift apart. The range is validated at the setter, not at the frame, because a zero or inverted range reconstructs positions of NaN from a depth normalised by the far plane -- a frame that renders and is silently wrong. A pass given no camera copies its input through, the same contract a missing depth image gets. |
| MOD-2006 | `isSupported()` as the two-part question (`MOD-1699`) | ✅ | The two-part question is the base class's and needed nothing here; **what this row actually found is that the second half of its own acceptance criterion was impossible.** `isSupported` takes a `GraphicsDevice` and nothing else, so it cannot see a frame's inputs -- a pass with no depth image, no normals and no camera is still *supported*, and it is `apply` that copies the input through. `docs/cnaext-engine-layer.md` had claimed the opposite of `SsaoPass` since Phase 5, which matters: a game that gates its prepass on `isSupported()` gets `true` and then wonders why the effect does nothing. The document now carries a two-row table splitting the questions -- can this renderer run the pass (the device, `isSupported`) versus does this frame carry what it needs (the context, `apply`) -- and two tests pin it so the sentence and the code cannot drift apart again. |
| MOD-2007 | Tests: hit, miss, rejection, fade, roughness, settings, unsupported | ✅ | **22 tests**, every one of them written alongside the behaviour it describes rather than after it. Hit, no-hit on a plane alone, camera-facing surface, both spellings of an empty depth pixel, all three rejections (backface, thickness, edge fade), roughness against smoothness, step-count independence, every accessor with its clamping, the two-part support question, and four separate copy-through cases -- no inputs, depth without normals, no camera, and zero intensity. Two of them are anti-vacuity guards for others: `ZeroIntensityReproducesTheSceneExactly` and the `ASSERT_GT` in front of every A/B comparison, because two misses agree perfectly and prove nothing. |
| MOD-2008 | `docs/cnaext-engine-layer.md`: SSR section, including what screen-space reflection cannot do | ✅ | A section stating the two limits first, before any setting: **only what is already on screen can be reflected** -- a surface facing away, an object outside the viewport, anything behind nearer geometry has no colour in the source image, and no tuning moves that, so a scene needing off-screen reflections wants an environment map instead; and **the depth image is silent about thickness**, which is what `thickness` stands in for. Then the placement and its two reasons, a settings table whose third column is the *failure* each wrong value produces, where roughness comes from and why its default is a mirror, and why the step count sharpens the reflection rather than moving it. |
| MOD-2009 | Verify the march against a real `DepthNormalPrepass` rather than synthetic images | ✅ | **Answered, and the answer is yes -- `cnaViewPositionFromDepth` agrees with the camera the prepass was driven with.** A quad is drawn in the +X +Y quadrant of the view through a real prepass, and at every pixel the depth says the quad occupies, the reconstructed position is asked whether it is in that same quadrant: 0 disagreements, with an anti-vacuity guard so a run that drew nothing fails instead of reporting perfect agreement. The render-target flip (`MOD-2000` finding 1) does **not** break it, because rasterising into a target and sampling it back apply the same flip, which cancels.
